2026-10-10 — **[neural settings: the 13-fit comparison]** (Codex working copy, branch `codex/t1-tuning-fixes`, `results/fakedata/nn_compare_gpu.tsv`, copied to `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/codex-compare-20261010/`; tuning replicate 1, 10,000 types, 10M training tokens, Sol's two stopping changes applied throughout). Fitted minus same-test oracle, 8 epochs minus 5: mapped lag 1 at k = 1, −0.021 bits (0.140 to 0.119); mapped lag 1 at k = 4, −0.024 (0.180 to 0.156); mapped lag 3 at k = 4, −0.019 (0.175 to 0.157); copy lag 1 at k = 1, −0.0001 (0.0553 to 0.0552); copy lag 3 at k = 4, +0.009 (0.083 to 0.092; `unigram_cosine3` 0.079); independent draws at k = 4, identical (both keep step zero, +0.0008). The rule fixed before the run ("choose 8 epochs if the mapped cells improve and the copy cells are no worse; otherwise keep 5") gives **5 epochs**, since one copy cell is worse with 8. So the settings the evidence points to are `zero_unigram_cosine5` with the two stopping changes, **for Brett to confirm**. Disclosed with them: on copy lag 1 at k = 1 with 10,000 types the neural model is +0.055 bits from the same-test oracle under either cap, just over F1's 0.05 tolerance, where KenLM is +0.040 at 10M tokens (+0.029 at 30M; `results/fakedata/f1v2_long_oracle.tsv`); on skipped dependencies it stays far closer than KenLM.

2026-10-10 — **Review of Claude's own edits** (Codex, read-only, `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-claude-edits-20261010/`). The Italian list's selection is now right: an independent parse of all 113,404 Italian IPA records found no false inclusions or exclusions under the every-pronunciation rule beyond two multiword entries (*agli occhi di*, *ti amo*) that got in because the parser deleted the IPA's word boundaries. But the review found defects in how Claude's edits are applied: (1) the protected pairs are numbered by Wiktionary's nuclei and applied to Epitran's, which differ (abbreviations such as *BMW* raise errors; 64 pairs point to nonadjacent Epitran nuclei, so *questuare* counts 5 instead of 4; Epitran drops vowels Wiktionary keeps, *sciare*); (2) Claude's tally span fix assumed a token overlapping no source token holds no syllable, which is false (g2p-en reads a lone 、 as four syllables) and dropping it changes adjacency for CAT-B; (3) Stage 2's gap marker ⟂ (where digits and out-of-inventory text were removed; about 20% of the tally sample's lines carry one) entered the tally as text and was read aloud by some G2Ps, which inflates totals and invents adjacency; (4) the sampler's stride truncates the tail of each source (here it still covers 99.2–99.97% of every source, so the bias is negligible, but the code is wrong); (5) the exposure counts are proxies, not upper bounds, and their Han denominator used Unicode names instead of the counters' Script=Han; (6) the span regression test passes when every comparison is skipped; plus latent defects (case-sensitive grouping before lowercasing; the lower tie bar U+035C; marked glides). Plan: Claude fixes the sampler (evenly spaced lines; gaps as phrase boundaries, the "split" reading of the gap fork) and the exposure script and redraws the sample; Codex fixes the counter, lexicon and tally defects and makes the pipeline honour the gap fork (split and drop); the corpus tally is then rerun and the summary relabelled. The tally figures logged earlier stand as provisional until then.

2026-10-10 — **Catalan function words, decided by Brett** (Roughdraft comment on `notes/cat-function-words-review-2026-10-10.md`): drop *jo* (a strong personal pronoun, lexically stressed); add the unstressed possessives *ta*, *sa* (and *sos*), which the IEC lists in the paradigm *mon, ton, son; ma, ta, sa*; leave *o* out as variable (Lloret lists *i, ni, si, que* as unstressed and identifies *o* as varying); keep *si* as the conjunction default with its limitation stated; remove the multiword *per a que*. He also pointed to a more direct source than inferring unstressedness from missing stress marks: the explicit `unstressed_words` table of English Wiktionary's `Module:ca-IPA` (revision 91037427, 2026-06-01, the current revision when checked; table read 2026-10-10). That table already has every one of his changes (*ta*, *sa*, *sos* in; *jo* out) except that it includes *o*; so the list becomes that table minus *o*, which also adds the contractions *al*, *als*, *del*, *dels*, *pel*, *pels*, *nos* and the Balearic articles *ets*, *so* that the first criterion missed (Claude's implementation of his comment; for Brett to confirm). Limitations to state: matching is by spelling, so homographs take the function-word reading (*te* also 'tea', *sa* also 'healthy', *si* the reflexive pronoun and a note name); and Brett's comment notes that an unstressed final vowel isn't automatically deletable, since the literature distinguishes deletion, gliding and retained hiatus, which joins the pending check of CAT-B against Wheeler or Hualde (now also Lloret).

That 0.05 was the original F1 version 2 rule, which KenLM also failed (fitted minus oracle at k = 1 with 10,000 types: 0.065–0.113 for independent draws) and which the revision Brett confirmed on 2026-10-09 replaced, for work after that date, with three checks and no numerical bound: (1) is the code right; (2) the estimator's cost, reported with its sampling variation; (3) whether it matters downstream, read off F3b. 2026-10-10 — **F3b with words and rate-based inventories** (decisions A1 and A2; Codex, branch `codex/t2-f3b-fixes`, 9575904; its implementation plan approved by Brett in Roughdraft, "plan approved", 12:42 UTC; 44 tests pass, 17 new, rerun here). Word lengths follow F1c's stationary process (1–4); boundaries are independent of syllable identity; each family's dependency continues across words; truth at 14 rungs including 1w; R_4 − R_1w reported beside the earlier contrasts; inventories assigned by rate rank after S0/S1 rates are built; targets are line-averaged true rung-4 densities. The full KenLM grid (four families × three pairings × 17 languages × 14 rungs, about 2,960 fits, estimated 55 hours run serially, 2.4–2.75 GB peak disk each) runs as four parallel processes, one per family, from 2026-10-10 13:20 UTC (free disk 161 GB). 2026-10-10 — Review of the F3b rework before its full grid (Codex, read-only, `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-f3bwords-20261010/`; side agent's note that the 14-hour run was on unreviewed code). Correct: every rung's truth matches exhaustive enumeration (168 cases, within 3.3e-15 bits), rung 1w resets at word starts as the Stage 4 encoding does, S0/S1 rates are built before inventories are assigned by rate rank (correlations 0 and −0.8, SD ratio 0.9), metrics and contrasts use the same rates (all 336 summary rows and 144 contrasts rechecked), and the `-inf` backoff fix hides no zero-probability case. Three problems: the full grid reused 54 seeds of the worker's small grid (the seed function ignores the run); four parallel processes aren't safe (the disk guard measures the shared directory, so three of the four runs stopped at once on "Disk stop" with 161 GiB free, and four KenLM fits at `-S 30%` each overcommit memory); and plain and boundary rungs score unseen syllables differently, so rows with `oov_tokens > 0` can't enter the rung comparison. The grid was stopped (the one surviving run had reached the third language of its family); its partial outputs are kept in `results/fakedata/abandoned-20261010-seedreuse/` on that branch and are not used. A fix round (`codex-t2parallel-20261010/`) adds a run seed namespace, per-process disk caps with a shared reservation lock, a configurable KenLM memory share, and automatic exclusion of OOV rows; the grid is relaunched after it.

2026-10-10 — Scope of the cut above (Claude, after a side agent's note; the treatment of the uncovered rows is for Brett). The tally measures how many syllables each line gets, so the 0.05% rule can only judge readings it measured by count. Two groups fall outside it. (a) Readings that move syllable boundaries, ownership or phones without changing counts (rows 45–53, and consonant placement in general, which the note already fixes before Stage 5 with maximal onset): these change syllable types and so the entropy, and need a measure on types (for example unigram and bigram syllable entropy on the tally sample) once segmentation exists. (b) Readings with no implemented alternative, skipped by the tally (rows 10, 11, 18, 20, 25, 28, 30, 31, 33, 38, 40, 41, 43): unmeasured, not negligible. The note's "every option enters the multiverse" line now states the rule and its limit. Also: the Vietnamese tally produced no comparisons (every one failed whole under the earlier code); it is rerun on the current code.

2026-10-10 — **Brett: three decisions** ("yes to all three"; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude). (1) Readings that move syllable boundaries without changing counts (rows 45–53, with consonant placement) are measured on syllable types, as unigram and bigram syllable entropy on the tally sample, once the pipeline segments syllables; readings with no implemented alternative (rows 18, 25, 28, 30, 31, 33, 38, 40, 41) keep their defaults and are reported as not measured, not as negligible. (2) Row 13 is frozen at the prevalence default (highest `10_FreqMot`); its alternative, the rarest attested entry, is a bound and is reported as one (0.053% of French A's syllables). (3) The neural F1 on seeds 2 and 3 is queued to start when seed 1 finishes.

2026-10-10 — Rows 29 and 37 (Vietnamese) checked after a side agent's note (Claude; classification for Brett). The tally implements both alternatives with one branch (`reading_tally.py`, `if row in {29, 37}`): every whitespace-delimited unit counts as one syllable. So their identical −315 of 151,226 is one measurement reported twice, and their 748 failed lines are units the shared tokenizer gives no token (attribution fails). Row 29's alternative counts a hyphenated pair of written syllables as one, against the written-syllable unit VIE-A counts; row 37's counts a symbol-only token as a syllable, and in the digit-free primary sample (no line has a digit) reading digits into words is row 38's secondary run. Neither is a reading anyone proposes. Claude recommends treating both like the fixed rows (the alternative is a defect), which would leave five forks: 16, 26, 12, 23, 17.

2026-10-10 — Correction (Claude, after a side agent's note): rows 5 and 23 were classified the wrong way round under the cut. The design points (B2, B3; `notes/design-points-explained-2026-10-10.md`, accepted) called the tally's figures artefacts for row 5 under FIN C′ and for row 23; Claude then excluded row 5 as a whole and kept row 23 as a fork. From the tally's affected types (`data/interim/tally-results-v2/reading-tally-{FIN,ITA,SPA,TUR}.tsv`, `top_types`): row 5's C′ effect (1.4%) is on words with no identical vowels (*kilometriä*, *pian*, *alkaen*), so an artefact; but outside C′ the row moves the count above the cutoff on genuine identical runs, FIN D 0.18% (*liian*, *maailman*, *tosiaan*), FIN A–C 0.07% (*maailman*), and in ITA B 0.25% and SPA B 0.10% mostly on short words (*a*, *e*, *una*), apparently identical vowels meeting across a word boundary. Row 23's 0.21% is on words with no deleted ğ (*saat*, *faaliyet*, *tabii*, Roman numerals), so its genuine effect is unmeasured. Applying Brett's rule to the genuine figures: row 5 is a fork (code's reading the default, per B2), and row 23 joins the unmeasured rows (default kept, reported as not measured). For Brett to confirm, with rows 29 and 37 (entry above).

2026-10-10 — **Brett: go ahead with the five steps to the first real count** (decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude). (1) The five forks (rows 16, 26, 12, 17, 5) become named, tested counter options (Codex). (2) Syllable segmentation by maximal onset, with each language's legal onsets taken from consonant clusters that begin words in its own corpus, with a small frequency floor (Codex writes and tests the tool on invented data; Claude runs it on the corpus locally; the floor's value and its sensitivity are reported, not chosen from results). (3) The reviewed Codex branches are imported into this repository through the redaction check, with tests rerun. (4) Today's decisions are published to the public pre-registration (r9). (5) Then the counters are frozen and the freeze timestamped, which lifts the NS hold.

2026-10-10 — **Conflict with the NS hold** (Claude, after a side agent's note; for Brett). Brett's decision (1) of the three today, measuring the boundary rows (45–53) by unigram and bigram syllable entropy on the tally sample, would compute real-corpus syllable entropy before the freeze, which the hold bars; the exception Brett granted covers the tally of syllable counts, not entropy, and bigram syllable entropy is close to the paper's ID. Paused: no syllable entropy is computed on any real text. Codex's T6 writes and tests the tool on invented words only, which the hold allows. Building the onset inventory from word-initial clusters in the corpus is a count of phonological structure, not entropy or NS, and is taken to fall under the tally exception unless Brett says otherwise. Options for Brett: measure the boundary rows by a count instead (the share of syllable tokens whose string changes, with the fork threshold fixed before the run); make every boundary row with a defined alternative a fork without measuring; or extend the exception to entropy explicitly.

2026-10-10 — **Brett: boundary rows measured by a count, not entropy** ("OK, sounds good", to Claude's recommendation; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude). Replacing decision (1)'s entropy measure: each boundary row (45–53) with a defined alternative is measured on the tally sample as the share of syllable tokens whose syllable string changes under the alternative; it is a fork if that share is 0.05% or more in any language, otherwise frozen at its default. Fixed before any run. No syllable entropy is computed on real text before the freeze; the entropy tool runs on invented text only until then. The onset inventory from word-initial clusters in the corpus falls under the tally exception (confirmed).

2026-10-10 — **[neural F1, check (2), seed 1]** 84 of 84 fits (`results/fakedata/f1_neural_final.tsv`, run 0450479c04bdd9be, Codex working copy 9e90a6f, Apple GPU, 14:28–20:26 UTC; `zero_unigram_cosine5` with the two stopping changes; early stopping in 36 fits). Fitted minus same-test oracle: near zero (under 0.005 bits) wherever the context is shorter than the process's lag, since there is then nothing beyond frequencies to learn; where the dependency is in reach it grows with the inventory and with k: copy processes 0.020–0.038 (600 types), 0.034–0.061 (2,000), 0.056–0.088 (10,000); mapped processes 0.035–0.057, 0.068–0.100 and 0.142–0.181; independent draws 0.001 or less. Largest 0.181 (mapped lag 1, 10,000 types, k = 4). No bound applies (check (2) reports cost; F3b reads its consequences). Seeds 2 and 3 (model seeds 1 and 2) running from 20:26 UTC; across-seed variation reported when they finish.

2026-10-10 — **Codex consulted on the claim-rule question** (Brett: "consult codex on that questoin"; gpt-6.1-sol, read-only, payload staged outside the repository, transcript audited; `private/reviews/consult-claimrule-20261010/`). Advice, not yet adopted: pre-register estimation reporting with rules that govern the prose as well as the figures (Gelman & Stern 2006: "the problem noted here is not solved simply by using confidence intervals"), no power gate (power needs an assumed true contrast; the observed one would be circular), no equivalence claims without a pre-registered margin, and R_k − 1 kept separate from the change Δ (a positive Δ can leave R below 1). Before the extension runs: investigate the intervals' undercoverage (null exclusion 13.4–16.2% against a nominal 10%; the 17-language population interval for S2's contrast covers 81.0%, below the 83–88% that `notes/multiverse-spec.md` states, checked against `f3a_design_17.md`); say what the document bootstrap covers (training variability isn't in it); finish the fitted-entropy checks. Design table: judge individual R against 1, not 0; add unconditional sign-error rates; relabel the redrawn S0/S1 exclusion rates; relabel the population interval as conditional on treating the languages as exchangeable. A multilevel model as a candidate sensitivity analysis, not a replacement. Its draft pre-registration text is in the report.

2026-10-10 — **Brett: a panel draw that leaves a language with no readings is left out and counted** ("ok, 1"; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude). Like undefined population draws, such draws are omitted jointly across paired quantities and their count is reported; computation stops only if they exceed 1% of draws. This replaces §4b's "stops the computation", which with the real pattern of missing readings would halt the analysis (one such draw in about 160,000 simulated ones). It conditions on every language having a reading, negligibly at that rate. Considered and set aside: redrawing (statistically the same), resampling texts within languages (breaks the joint draw over translated texts), and a weighted (Bayesian) bootstrap (would need recalibration; following Kilgarriff 2005, the sparsity is in the readings and no resampling scheme supplies data that don't exist). Kilgarriff's other point, that the sampling unit is the document, is already in §4b; test sets are kept generous so the document component stays small.

The 600-line probe behind the fallbacks proposal misread (a) and (c) as words with no pronunciation and (b) as length marks. Proposed to Brett as defect fixes, not readings: a leading ン or ッ is treated like a leading ー (attached to the preceding syllable across the word boundary, dropped at line start; it adds no syllable either way); a token of punctuation or symbols only is non-syllabic (zero syllables, no reader consulted), and a pause mark in a reader's output is stripped; (d) stays a reported failure. Queued as T14 with the Thai findings.

2026-10-11 — **Brett adopts the external review of the build plan and the readings** ("adopt the review, accept las, follow your recommendation"; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude; the review was pasted by Brett, written by another model that had not seen the repository; Claude checked its sources for the readings first: Euskaltzaindia rule 35, the National Institute of Korean Language answer of 2026-02-12, Kotus on inflecting numbers read aloud, TUFS on Japanese long vowels; DLTV and words.hk not checked). The decisions:
- (1) Same text across options: one removal mask per language, the union of declared data and alignment failures over every included option; software faults (a missing model, a broken dependency, a resource failure) stop the run instead.
- (2) Context resets at a dropped line; the gap is kept explicit.
- (3) The mojibake rule, with its predicate, processing order and the denominator of the 10% fixed, and checked against legitimate Thai for overmatching.
- (4) Word segmenters for CMN, YUE and VIE are forks (jieba or pkuseg; PyCantonese or cantoseg; underthesea or pyvi), with their models and dictionaries pinned as well as package versions.
- (5) Plateau: report Δ<sub>k</sub> = H<sub>k</sub>(B/2) − H<sub>k</sub>(B) at each rung with the learning curve, the corresponding change in the headline contrast and paired uncertainty where the design supplies it; 0.05 bits is only an operational warning flag, not a test of saturation; B/4 optional.
- (6) Working default item 2 confirmed: held-out 0 and 1w; the B/10 refits brought into line (done in P5); plug-ins identified separately.
- (7) The Thai alignment default, with an exact reconstruction rule (the normalisation and equivalences allowed when checking that pieces reproduce the reading); its declared failures enter (1)'s mask.
- (8) Counts on the full corpora before the stamp: the preparation outputs needed for feasibility are generated, their final versions bound into the stamp; the pre-stamp cost benchmark uses simulated data at production scale.
- (9) The secondary run kept as a sensitivity analysis using specified readings, the readings described as chosen conventions.
- Build specifications tightened: same text spans for every option, chosen under the first-listed convention, each option's realised syllable total reported; B from the training material after cleaning and the document split; B/2 nested in B with validation and test fixed; 3B as already approved (fix B₀, find the languages that reach 3B₀, compare B₀ with 3B₀ on that subset, B₀ unchanged); the same scored targets at every rung, or explicit accounting for boundary exclusions; the NS-closest rule settled before the freeze (which subset selects, how texts and languages weigh, ties, whether the secondary run reselects, leave-one-text-out reselecting), its reuse of NS reported; dictionary an alias in summaries and weights, never a second specification; the order counts, simulated benchmark, settled configuration, regenerated preparation outputs, archive and stamp, then entropy and NS; the freeze manifest covering corpus identities, models, dictionaries, dependency versions, seeds and the readings table; an estimate reused on resumption only when its data, code, resource and configuration identities match.
- Readings: both Basque readings kept (*zortziak hamar gutxitan*; *bost eta erdietarako*); the Korean pause kept (칠 육 이 오 팔 사로); the lengthened Japanese にーごーはちよん (same count under the counter, which attaches ー to its syllable); Spanish *las nueve* and *las cinco* restored (the first writer's readings: the criterion is the conventional spoken expansion, not what is printed); Thai clock-time spans extended to take in the printed abbreviation น., which the readings already voice as นาฬิกา; the Cantonese *o係* recorded as one span read 喺; telephone groupings recorded as chosen conventions with acceptable alternatives kept for a post-freeze sensitivity on the read-text counts; route and source status kept separate; candidate text in place of A/B in exported reasons.
- Claude's recommendations, adopted: vocabularies and onset inventories learned from training material only (onset inventories rebuilt from training documents, not the tally sample); and a source check of the readings by a verifier with reference access, isolated from NS and the analysis.

Four fresh Claude verifiers, each a subagent with web search and fetch but no access to local files, so they can't see NS or the analysis: EUS/FIN/HUN/TUR, CAT/FRA/ITA/SPA/ENG, JPN/KOR/CMN/YUE, THA/VIE (payloads `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/sourcecheck-20261011/payload-{A,B,C,D}.json`: id, token, about 25–45 characters of context each side, the current reading, its kind). 2026-10-11 — **Mojibake rule amended** (Brett, answering the open question with a pasted recommendation; decision owner Brett). The 10% threshold stays. The detector becomes the 2026-10-11 scan's (runs of เธ/เน for Thai; Ã/Â/â€/Ð/Ñ plus a continuation character for Latin), frozen as code, replacing the strict cp874/cp1252 round trip, which can't match most Thai mojibake. 2026-10-13 — **Brett: both P3 recommendations accepted** ("accept both recommendations"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude; `notes/p3-split-sizes-2026-10-13.md`). (1) Held-out sizes: the same in every language, test 1,000,000 and validation 500,000 syllables (first-listed option, after the common mask), filled by whole document groups in one seeded shuffle per language, test first, then validation, the rest training, realised sizes reported. (2) The line mask and the onset inventories are learned once per language from the whole training split; the budget forks (B, B/2, 3B) cut only the training text the models see, as a prefix in split order applied identically to every option; the vocabulary is learned from each budget's own training text, so the open OOV question gates B/2 as well as B.

2026-10-13 — **Brett: onset inventories learned from the same-size slice** (answering the question raised by a side agent via Brett; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude). The inventory floor counts how often a cluster occurs in training words, so learning from each language's whole training split would let larger corpora (SPA about 200 MB of subtitles, EUS about 31 MB) admit more rare clusters, and syllable cuts would depend on corpus size. Instead every language's onset inventories are learned from its B₀ training prefix (the common budget, the same size everywhere), then held fixed for B/2 and 3B; B₀ is planned first from syllable counts, which don't depend on the inventory. This refines the second P3 decision above; the mask is still one per language and inclusion set.

2026-10-13 — **Brett: B₀ is the minimum over every language and every text variant of a run** (decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, who raised it while building P7's counts phase). Text variants with less text than the first-listed settings (the Wikipedia stub at 0.3; Thai prose lines of 150 characters) can't reach a B₀ planned under first-listed settings alone, and the language that sets B₀ (probably YUE in the main run) would fail under them every time. So each run's B₀ is the smallest training total over all its languages and all their text variants, and every universe of the run trains on that amount; the comparison between text variants isn't confounded with budget, at the cost of a slightly smaller B₀. This refines "B set by the smallest language" (2026-10-08). Also noted while building P7 (side agent via Brett): the counts phase prepares only the text variants that apply to a language, so subtitle languages aren't prepared once per Wikipedia setting; assembly will treat a setting that doesn't apply to a language as not applicable rather than requiring duplicate jobs.

2026-10-13 — **Brett: cross-line context also resets where Stage 2 dropped a line** ("yes, I think context should reset there too"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, who raised it while building P7). Decision 2 (2026-10-11) reset context at lines the common mask drops; Stage 2's own filters (music, credits, digits, letters, English, mojibake) also drop lines, and the readers joined their neighbours. Now a dropped Stage 2 line resets context too under cross-line treatment, so no adjacency appears that the source doesn't have. Line-bounded treatment is unaffected.

Also corrected in P7: a Wikipedia article's split group is its Stage 2 row group, as `src/stage2_wikipedia.py` declares, not the article.

2026-10-13 — **What the minimum over variants costs, measured roughly** (raised by a side agent via Brett: YUE's stub-0.3 variant probably sets the main run's B0). Count on `data/raw/wikipedia/YUE.tsv.gz` (Han characters, about one syllable each in Cantonese; a full-corpus count, not a pipeline syllable count; before the split, so held-out text is still included): stub 0.5 keeps 96,337 articles and 9,658,962 Han characters under the drop rule (17,541,055 under split); stub 0.3 keeps 87,327 articles and 9,536,670 (17,212,450). The 0.3 threshold drops 9.4% of articles but only 1.3% of the drop-rule text, since the stubs are short. So taking B0 over variants costs YUE about 1.3%, if YUE sets B0. The counts phase now reports B0 under first-listed settings beside the B0 used (P7, `B0_first_listed`), so the real figure will be in the run record.

2026-10-14 — **P7 piece 2: the freeze covers the production closure, its resources and its environment** (Claude, as adopted 2026-10-11; working-copy commits 7f19af5, bacf275, 71b7988). `freeze.CODE_FILES` is the 41 files the post-stamp routes (the production command and the B/10 refit tool) can execute, held equal by a test to a trace of every project file they name; the simulated end-to-end command executes only listed files (audit hook, fresh process). Every listed module retains its source. Validation follows module-valued attributes from listed modules, so a project module they hold but the list lacks fails, and the authorization loads the whole list first. The manifest (`production-freeze-v2`) adds eight resource files (the frozen lexicons and their provenance, the digit readings, the disk calibration; no configuration file is read by listed code) and the environment: Python, 23 third-party distributions (the segmenters' and readers' models and dictionaries ship in them), the KenLM binaries, espeak-ng, and the models listed code reads from outside the packages (the venv's NLTK data for English, the pinned Thai G2P model, attacut's version). A real run re-checks all of it before writing its entropy record. Left out, with reasons in `CODEX-LOG.md`: the counts-only tools (no entropy; the read-text guard refuses them on the read texts) and espeak-ng's data beyond its version string. The header changed four F1c-fingerprinted sources, so F1c's code ID is now c4c98f67369b, pinned to the families of c755e98dea83 after the existing regressions gave byte-equal scores on all 22 KenLM and 44 neural cells. The counts phase's outputs and corpus identities join the archive at the freeze itself (P10).

The piece-2 review (`private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-p7piece2-20261014/`, manifest there; transcript audit 16 exec blocks, 0 flagged) found three high gaps and one medium, all fixed (781d525): package data and outside data that can change with the same version are now recorded (each distribution's installed-file record, the segmenters' data files, the Japanese dictionaries, espeak-ng's data, attacut's whole interpreter); the check now follows functions, containers and project-class instances, so a stale function kept in a registry fails; resources read into memory must match the listed files; and the runner, the refit tool, the NS lens and `segment_entropy.analyze` re-check at the end, as the production command does. 2026-10-14 — **The freeze is tied to the machine it is written on** (side agent via Brett; Claude confirms and adds the cause). The environment record holds this Mac's Python build, the KenLM and espeak-ng binaries, and data paths in the home folder, so a stamp made here refuses a run elsewhere. The code itself already hard-codes these paths (`runner.KENLM`, `counters.ESPEAK`, `thai.MODEL`, `thai.ATTACUT_PYTHON`), so a cloud run would need code changes, and so a new freeze, in any case. Consequences for P9 and P10: the local-or-cloud decision (P9, already before the stamp) also fixes where the freeze record is written; if the run goes to Modal or another cloud machine, its paths are set in the code and the record is written on that machine's image before the stamp. Between the stamp and the run, nothing on the run machine is upgraded (Homebrew can update espeak-ng as a side effect of other installs; pinning it, `brew pin espeak-ng`, is Brett's call).

2026-10-14 — **P8 rehearsal finding: under FRA-B, every French line with a schwa-only clitic is masked** (Claude; open for Brett). The first real-mode rehearsal on invented text stopped at B₀ = 70 syllables, set by French: 48 of its 96 lines were masked, all under FRA-B's two declarations, all "Words without counted nuclei must not be silently dropped" (`segment.py`). FRA-B deletes the schwa of *je*, *de*, *le*, *que*, *ne*, *me*, *te*, *se*, which leaves those words no nucleus; nothing places their consonants, so segmentation refuses the line and the common mask drops it from all four French options. Checked through the pipeline on invented lines: *je*, *que*, *ne*, *me* and *le* fail, a line without them counts. The 2026-10-10 tally didn't see it because it counted without cutting, and the tests' French has no such clitics. Scale (`results/p8/fra-schwa-clitics/`, counts only): 41.1% of the first 200,000 lines of the Stage 2 French subtitles hold one of these words (82,164 lines; *je* 29,230, *de* 27,338, *le* 19,246, *que* 17,482). As the code stands, the real counts phase would drop those lines, biasing French's text towards lines without them, and French would likely set B₀. This is the "how French consonants are placed when deleting a schwa" question left open on 2026-10-10, now for whole words. Options for Brett: (a) the clitic's consonants join the consonant stretch between the neighbouring nuclei and are cut by the existing rules (largest legal onset, the declared ownership fork for a syllable that spans words), the word recorded as a member of the syllable they join; no new fork, and FRA-B's counts stay as tallied (Claude's recommendation); (b) a fixed rule: they join the next word's first syllable (the previous word's last at the end of a line); simpler, but ignores onset legality (*je sais* gives the onset [ʒs]); (c) FRA-B keeps the schwa in monosyllabic clitics, which changes FRA-B's counts from those tallied; (d) leave it, and lose about 41% of French lines. The real counts phase waits for this. The rehearsal continues with invented French lines without these clitics, to exercise the rest of the real path, and reruns with the original lines after the fix.

2026-10-14 — **Brett: under FRA-B, a schwa-only clitic's consonants are cut by the existing rules** (decision owner Brett; assisting Claude; option (a) of the entry above). They join the consonant stretch between the neighbouring nuclei and are cut there by the rules already in place (largest legal onset; the declared ownership fork for a syllable that spans words), and the word is recorded as a member of the syllable they join. No new fork; FRA-B's counts stay as tallied on 2026-10-10.

2026-10-14 — **The FRA-B clitic rule, stated exactly** (Claude; correcting the description Brett approved, after a side agent's note via Brett; for Brett to confirm). "The cut rules already in place" was inaccurate: the code cuts each French word on its own, by Lexique's syllables, and has never cut consonants across a word boundary. What is implemented (`segment._french_clitic_attachments`, working-copy branch `p8-fra/20261014`) is a new rule for these words only, built from existing parts: take the consonants between the last nucleus before the clitic and the first nucleus after it; find the longest final run of them that is a legal onset, i.e. an onset that begins words in the training text (the learned inventory, as for word-internal cuts); if the clitic's consonant falls inside that run it joins the next word's first syllable, otherwise the previous word's last. At the start of a line it joins the next syllable, at the end the previous one. *Il me parle* gives [ilm.paʁl], *sur le pont* [syʁl.pɔ̃], *tu ne viens* [tyn.vjɛ̃], *le chat dort* [lʃa.dɔʁ]. The word becomes a member of that syllable, so the ownership fork decides who owns it; syllable types include the attached consonant; counts are unchanged. A line made only of such words (*Que ?*) still has no nucleus and stays masked: 111 of the 200,000 lines measured (0.06%), against 41.1% of lines lost without the rule (`results/p8/fra-schwa-clitics/`).

2026-10-14 — **Brett confirms the FRA-B clitic rule as stated in the entry above** (decision owner Brett; asked again after the correction): onset-based placement across the word boundary for schwa-only clitics only, line edges to the one neighbour, clitic-only lines still masked.

2026-10-14 — **Which real lines the common mask would drop, measured on a sample before the real counts run** (raised by a side agent via Brett: syllable cutting had never run on real text; Claude; counts only, no entropy, no read text). `results/p8/real-mask-sample/` runs every registry option, with the FRA-B clitic rule (working copy 3d8e901) and empty onset inventories as the counts phase does, on whole documents streamed from each language's Stage 2 file to about 3,000 lines (2 to 8 subtitle files per language, so a narrow sample; YUE 591 Wikipedia articles). Share of lines a failure would drop from all of a language's options: CAT 0.0%, CMN 1.1%, DEU 0.0%, ENG 0.1%, EUS 0.0%, FIN 0.4%, FRA 0.8%, HUN 1.4%, ITA 1.5%, JPN 0.1%, KOR 1.0%, SPA 0.0%, SRP 3.0%, THA 0.4%, TUR 0.4%, VIE 0.1%, YUE 2.7%. The causes: (1) tokens with no vowel that aren't clitics: abbreviations and initials (*M.*, *Mr*, *Mrs*, single letters), interjections (*shh*, *hm*, *mm*), the French euphonic *-t-* (*a-t-il*) and the like, the bulk of SRP 3.0%, ITA 1.5%, HUN 1.4%, FRA 0.8% (now under FRA-A), TUR and FIN; (2) transliterated names joined by the interpunct · in CMN (1.2%) and YUE (2.7%): the characters have readings but the · doesn't, and the tokenizer keeps it inside the word; (3) Korean G2P output that isn't Hangul (KOR 1.0%); (4) a few Thai and Japanese readings. Open for Brett: whether · (U+00B7) should separate words in CMN and YUE only (Catalan writes it inside words, *col·legi*), which would keep lines with foreign names (Claude's recommendation, as the loss is systematic); and whether to leave lines with vowelless non-clitic tokens dropped (Claude's recommendation: small, and an abbreviation isn't pronounced as written) or extend the clitic rule to them.

2026-10-14 — **The real counts run's cost, measured and cut** (raised by a side agent via Brett; Claude). Measured on the real-text sample (`results/p8/real-mask-sample/timing.txt`, `timing-after.txt`): cutting cost 5.6–6.3 ms per line and option with a warm cache, so one pass over the 88 million Stage 2 subtitle lines in every option (674 million line-options; ITA alone 241 million, from its 36 options) would take about 47 days on one thread, and the counts phase makes about five passes (the mask, every text variant at every budget, NS-closest). A profile put 84% of the time in a fingerprint recomputed on every word lookup; caching it (working copy 72b9ee1, results unchanged, full suite passes) brings warm cutting to about 0.6 ms. That makes one pass about 4.7 days on one thread, the run about 23 thread-days, or about 3 days in 8 processes, plus a one-time G2P cost for each new word. Further cuts, for Brett with P9: (1) run languages in parallel (code only); (2) process only the documents the budgets can reach plus the held-out ones, which would cut the passes after the first several-fold, but sizing the split from unmasked first-listed counts would be a small change to how the split is made; (3) reuse each line's counter result across passes, since counting doesn't depend on the onset inventory (code only).

2026-10-14 — **Brett: in CMN and YUE only, the interpunct · separates words; other vowelless tokens stay dropped** (decision owner Brett; assisting Claude; the two questions of the mask-sample entry above). Transliterated names joined by · (U+00B7) are read part by part instead of masking the line (CMN 1.1%, YUE 2.7% of sampled lines); Catalan keeps · inside words. Lines holding vowelless tokens other than FRA-B's clitics (abbreviations and initials, interjections such as *shh*, the French euphonic *-t-*) remain masked from all of a language's options (at most 3.0% of sampled lines, SRP), and the share dropped is reported.

2026-10-14 — **Brett: in CMN and YUE, ·, ・, ‧ and • all separate words** (decision owner Brett; refines the entry above). Besides · (U+00B7), Chinese and Cantonese text joins name parts with ・ (U+30FB; 463 of 300,000 sampled CMN lines, against 561 with ·) and ‧ (U+2027; 128 of 300,000 YUE lines), and a decorative bullet • (U+2022) appears in 139 CMN and 112 YUE lines; each would otherwise drop its line.

2026-10-14 — **`prepare` as built can't take real corpora: three full passes per budget, and every report held in memory** (Claude; found while sizing P9). Each preparation passes over all of a language's documents in every option three times: a provisional mask (the counts phase's mask again), the mask with learned onsets, and per-document reports. The onset inventories come from the B₀ prefix, the same for every budget label of a text variant, so those passes are identical for B, B/2, 3B and NS-closest; a subtitle language makes about 14 full passes, not the 5 estimated above. And `prepare` holds every document's reports for a declaration at once: 19 KB per line on real German lines (41 KB at peak), about 125 GB for German's 6.6 million lines. Also: the 124 declarations use 62 counter configurations (each pair differs only in a boundary setting), so counting each line once per configuration halves the counting. Plan, code-only and result-neutral (checked against the rehearsal's per-preparation outputs before and after, then a read-only outside review): one scan per text variant reused by every budget label and by NS-closest, the counts phase's mask passed in as the provisional mask, the first-listed declaration over all documents (so `BUDGETS.verify` is unchanged) and the others only over the documents the largest cut and the held-out sets use, one mask per variant over that union, streaming per document with only compact labels kept, a counter result shared by declarations that differ only in boundary settings, and languages in parallel worker processes. The counts phase's own mask (every line, every option, about 4.7 thread-days at 0.6 ms) remains the floor.

2026-10-14 — **Review of the P8 fixes returned; the cache's integrity restored** (Codex read-only, gpt-6.1-sol, high, on 781d525..5c4e23d; `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-p8fixes-20261014/`, manifest there; transcript audit 16 exec blocks, 0 flagged). Sound in its tests: the FRA-B clitic rule (runs of clitics, both line edges, Lexique prefixes and suffixes, ownership and word-initial flags, unchanged counts, FRA-A untouched, and placement differing between empty and learned inventories without changing which lines are masked), the CMN and YUE name dots on every path, and the real-run backend refusals. Two P2 defects, both in the speed-up and both fixed by Claude (working copy f30f5ea, with regressions): a cache record or source file rewritten with the same size and a preserved modification time was reused where the old code refused it (file stamps now hold the change time and inode too), and shared pronunciations were mutable values a caller could poison (now immutable strings). The speed-up holds (profile sample 5.3 s against 120 s originally). 2026-10-14 — **The counts phase runs languages in parallel, each in a fresh process** (Claude; code-only, result-neutral; working copy 1793538). A per-document pool of forked workers was tried first and dropped: on macOS the children crashed in the Objective-C runtime's fork-safety check (the parent was multi-threaded) and the pool waited on them indefinitely. Now `counts_phase.run` works per language in two stages around B₀ (`measure_language`: the split and every variant's counts; `prepare_language`: every variant and budget), and with `INFO_RATE_WORKERS` above 1 runs that many languages at once, each a new `python -I -B` process with JSON arguments and results; both routes pass results through JSON, so the record doesn't depend on the number of workers. The parent no longer holds every variant's corpus until preparation (68 Wikipedia variants at once). Checked on the rehearsal with failing lines, 8 workers against the sequential run, by content: 0 differences in 67 (65 preparations, 2 budget plans); 124 s against 227 s, with other runs sharing the machine. Each worker took 0.3 to 1.1 GB on these small inputs. A side agent's point, adopted for P9: the run can't finish faster than its largest single language, since each language runs on one core, so P9 projects the Catalan timing to the largest language before local or cloud is decided, and more cores stop helping once every language of a run has one (17; the main and Wikipedia runs go one after the other, and each stage waits for its slowest language).

2026-10-15 — How NS were obtained: consultants counted the canonical number of syllables in each text and didn't provide a syllabification (F. Pellegrino, personal communication, October 2026). Permission to cite this as a personal communication was given in October 2026; it covers this procedure only. Placed in the public record by Brett's decision (2026-10-15), to go out with the next regular push.

2026-10-15 — **Mask fixes imported** (7a21b18, from the working copy 35bd823..7927d3c; main matched the working copy's base 280aaf9 file for file before the copy). A removal mask is validated once per pass (`pipeline.ValidatedMask`), and masks use schema `stage4-language-mask-v3`: each declaration's distinct configurations stored once in a table, documents holding indices. Two read-only Codex reviews (`review-p9mask-20261015`, `review-p9maskv3-20261015`), three findings fixed. Masked lines, reports and training text unchanged; the masks' own digests change with the format. Main suite: 80 files, 947 tests, 0 failures, 2 skipped. The G2P-cache and syllable-basis fix (batch item 9, approved by Brett today) follows separately.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: a pass/fail check of the real read texts before the stamp** (batch item 5a, "a"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude; raised by a side agent 2026-10-14). The real read texts go through the production path under every option before the stamp; only pass or fail and the failure's reason class are kept for each language, option and text. No syllable count, pronunciation or ratio is kept or shown, and the pronunciation cache sits in a temporary folder deleted afterwards. Why: `ns_lens.closest` refuses to rank a reduced set, so one failure on any text under any option leaves NS-closest undefined, and under the hold it would otherwise show only after the stamp. Fixes it prompts are post hoc (informed by pass/fail, not by NS) and logged. Brett asked whether a rule replacing an unexpected string with a normalized one is acceptable; Claude's answer: yes for strings the G2P can't read, if it maps to the spoken form, is fixed before the stamp and applied before any option counts, logs every firing, and is an explicit list of transformations; no for a convention's own rule failing (that needs a declared fallback in the counter). Item 5b (what is reported if NS-closest is still undefined) open.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: if NS-closest is undefined, report it as undefined with the failures listed, and the closest among the options that completed as a labelled secondary** (batch item 5b, "yes, undefined plus labelled secondary"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude). The secondary alone could mislead if the option that failed would have been closest, so it is never reported without the primary.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: held-out syllables unseen in training are counted first, then a pre-stated rule decides the policy** (batch item 1, "count first and then a pre-stated rule"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, with a read-only Codex view, `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-batch-20261015/`). Step 1: `prepare` collects, for every declaration and budget, the held-out syllable types and flag+syllable pairs unseen in training, their share of held-out tokens and the share of held-out lines they touch, and only then stops as now; counts only, on the post-fix Catalan run and a few large corpora (CMN, JPN, FIN Wikipedia). Step 2, the rule: under 0.1% of held-out tokens in every language and budget, one logged default; otherwise escape coding (the probability of a new syllable, with rare training syllables mapped to the unknown symbol for support, plus the bits to spell it from its segments) as the main analysis and the pooled unknown symbol as a labelled secondary, both reported. Line removal is not an option (it keeps the easier text). Which policy is the default under 0.1% is still to be confirmed by Brett.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: F2 keeps the synthetic processes and regroupings and adds a neural subset, costed before launch** (batch item 3, "keep and add neural subset, costed first"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, with a read-only Codex view, `review-batch-20261015`). Still open: matched spans of fine units as the second comparison (Codex and Claude recommend it over context matched in bits, with the span distribution reported for variable-length merges), an oracle on the same finite contexts and test realization, and identical fine-unit train and test material across regroupings.

2026-10-15 — The OOV rule (item 1, above) is applied to every language, text variant, declaration and budget before the stamp (raised by a side agent via Brett: the planned early count covers four corpora while the rule covers all). The counts phase runs on the full corpora before the stamp and its final outputs are bound into it (build plan, 2026-10-11, item 8), and `prepare` now records the unseen shares for every declaration and budget before stopping, so the full panel's counts exist before anything is frozen. The early count on Catalan, Mandarin, Japanese and Finnish Wikipedia is a warning so that escape coding can be built in time if it is needed. 2026-10-15 — **Brett: local or cloud is decided after measuring; putting the corpus text on a cloud provider is acceptable** (batch item 6, "defer on that basis. putting the text there is fine"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, with a read-only Codex view, `review-batch-20261015`). Measure first: the post-fix Catalan run and a memory probe on the largest Wikipedia articles (the longest document sets a worker's peak). Then: local if four or more workers fit in the laptop's 32 GB and the counts phase comes in under about a week on the clock; otherwise the counts phase locally and the entropy phase on one cloud machine with the freeze written there (Codex's option), or everything on one cloud machine if that doesn't work out. The subtitle text, or syllable encodings derived from it, may go to the provider.

2026-10-15 — **Brett on P2's other defaults** (batch item 2; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, with a read-only Codex view, `review-batch-20261015`). (1) The onset floor: measure, then a pre-stated rule ("measure plus rule"). The share of syllable tokens whose label changes between floors 1, 3 and 10 is counted (counts only) on the post-fix Catalan run and the early-count corpora; under 0.1%, floor 3 stays as the logged default; otherwise the floor becomes a fork and each value is reported. 2026-10-15 — **Token files exist only while their job runs** (Claude; Brett "ok"; approach Claude's under Brett's direction of today that the approach to result-neutral fixes is Claude's to pick). As built, `prepare` writes every rung's token files for every budget, declaration and treatment, each twice (per document and per split), and keeps them until the entropy phase. Measured on invented text with a 5,000-type vocabulary: 364 bytes per syllable for all rungs and both treatments (166 once identical files are written once), about 85 GB for Catalan alone and TB-scale for the whole run; encoding costs about 18 CPU s per million syllables for all rungs, about a tenth of the scan cost; a compact binary record of the scanned syllables is 1.5 to 1.7 bytes per syllable compressed. The fix (working copy, in progress): `prepare` records each token file's digest and size and a compact record per declaration; just before each entropy job its files are rebuilt from the record, checked by the runner's existing provenance check against the recorded digests, and deleted after. What the stamp binds and what the runner checks are unchanged. One Codex review. Until it is in, the pooled-symbol commits (working copy 0edac5f, d1f76e7) stay out of main: with them a full Catalan preparation would try to write about 85 GB into `data/`.

2026-10-15 — **OpenSubtitles' terms checked for cloud processing** (Claude; Brett's multiverse choice 4 of 2026-10-09 and item 6 of today). OPUS (https://opus.nlpl.eu/legacy/OpenSubtitles.php, read today) still names no licence and asks only for the opensubtitles.org link and the Lison & Tiedemann (2016) citation. OpenSubtitles' own pages at opensubtitles.org and opensubtitles.com refuse automated fetches (403), and the archive route is blocked, so the reading is from the OpenSubtitles help centre (https://opensubtitles.tawk.help/article/legal-information and /article/terms-of-service, each labelled as last updated about two years ago; copyright line 2006–2024): the documents are for "non-commercial, scientific and educational purposes" (the sentence on the page reads "may not be freely distributed and used for", which looks garbled), commercial use is protected by copyright and "prohibited", and nothing is said about where the text may be processed, about automated processing, or about machine learning. Reading: running our non-commercial analysis on a rented machine in our own account is processing for a scientific purpose, not distribution, so the terms don't stand in the way of item 6's cloud options. Conditions Claude will hold to: the text goes only to private storage in our account (never a public dataset or Space), it is deleted when the run's outputs are back, and nothing derived from it is published beyond what the data-release decisions of 2026-10-10 already allow. If Brett wants the live .org terms read before an upload, that needs Chrome connected or him opening the page.

2026-10-15 — **The onset floor crosses the 0.1% line in Catalan, and the learned inventories admit non-native onsets at every floor** (Claude's measurement, counts only; decision pending Brett). Script `results/p9/onset-floor/measure_onset_floor.py` (re-floors the B₀-prefix inventory that `prepare` learns and segments held-out test documents through `process_document`'s scan path; labels compared position by position); diagnostics in `results/p9/onset-floor/diagnostic/`. CAT-3's CAT-A inventory, smoke test on 2 test documents (5,600 syllables): floor 1 vs 3 changes 5.8% of syllable labels, 3 vs 10 3.4%, 1 vs 10 8.9%. So the rule Brett set this morning (under 0.1% floor 3 stays, otherwise a fork) gives a fork; the full test split for every Catalan declaration is running. The inventory itself is the larger problem: it has 101 multi-phone onsets, among them *st* (270 word types), *sp* (111), *sk* (107), *zl*, *sb*, *tb*, *kn* at 10 or more types and *mb*, *ld*, *ls*, *kt* at 3–9. They come from English and German names and words read with Catalan rules (*Stéphane*, *Swan*, *Knicks*, *twit*, *Schoenberg*), acronyms (*NBA*, *GTA*), elision slips and OCR's l for I (*lntente*, *ldiota*). OCR confusions are under 0.04% of tokens in every subtitle language, so they matter only through type counts. Catalan has no word-initial s+consonant, but with *st* legal a native word such as *aquesta* is cut before the cluster. Measured on 12 test documents (99,415 syllables, CAT-A): learning onsets only from word types listed in Catalan Wiktionary (`data/interim/wiktionary/ca.jsonl.gz`, words and forms) leaves 15 multi-phone onsets at floor 3 (*bl bɾ dɾ fl fɾ kl kw kɾ pl pɾ ts tɾ ɡl ɡw ɡɾ*) and changes 18.8% of labels against the inventory as built; counting only types attested in lowercase (dropping names) changes 3.9%. Within the lexicon-filtered inventory the floor still matters: 1 vs 3 changes 9.3% (floor 1 admits *pt*, *km*, *dn*), 3 vs 10 0.64% (only *ts* differs). Wiktionary exists for all 11 onset languages, but its size varies (Basque 18,243 entries, English 1.49 million). Question for Brett: which onset source.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: onset inventories learned from lexicon-listed word types; the as-built inventory and floors 1 and 10 become one-at-a-time sensitivity checks** ("yes", to Claude's recommendation; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude). Reason (Brett asked whether it matters for the question): the inventory and the floor move syllable boundaries within words but never the count, so speech rate is untouched; any fixed segmentation is a recoding of the same phone string, so with enough context the bits per syllable converge whatever the cut, and the choice shows at short context, which includes the paper's within-word bigram ID and our low rungs (with finite training text, wrong cuts also add rare syllable types, so the convergence isn't complete). Its size at each context length can't be measured before the stamp, since that needs real-corpus entropy. The distortion isn't random: it falls on languages whose native onsets differ from those of English names and loans (Catalan measured). So, as for the boundary rows on 2026-10-10: (1) a word type counts toward an onset's type count only if the language's Wiktionary lists it (headword or form), at floor 3; (2) the inventory as built (every training type) and floors 1 and 10 (on the lexicon-filtered inventory) are run against the default after the stamp, one at a time, not crossed with other forks, their changed-label shares measured and reported, deciding nothing. This replaces this morning's rule for the floor (a fork at 0.1% or more). Each language's lexicon coverage (types and training tokens it lists) is reported, and each inventory is looked at when its language is prepared, Basque's lexicon being the smallest (18,243 entries).

What was seen: onset inventories and counts of changed syllable labels on subtitle text, no entropy, no read text, no NS. The reason for the change is in the entry (the choice moves boundaries, never counts, so it shows at short context; the same treatment as the boundary rows of 2026-10-10), but it is a change after a result and is marked so in the record.

2026-10-15 — **Read-text pass/fail check run (batch item 5a)**: `results/p9/read-text-check/check_read_texts.py`, record `read-text-check.json` (pass or fail and reason classes only; counts dropped as each record is made, pronunciations in a temporary folder deleted on exit, the readings table opened for its Language and Text columns only). 255 read texts, 128 configurations (FIN-C included). Primary subset: 70 of 1,256 text-configuration records fail; secondary: 114 of 1,920; every failure is in a production configuration, so NS-closest would be undefined as things stand. Causes, from the failing tokens (Claude looked at the tokens, never at a count, pronunciation or NS): (1) ITA, every option, texts P0 and P9: Epitran reads word-final *gli* as /ʎ/ with no vowel (*gli* → ʎ, *figli* → fiʎ, *degli* → deʎ), so a standalone *gli* has no syllable and every other word in *-gli* loses its last one; in the Italian subtitles standalone *gli* is in 1.16% of lines (0.22% of tokens), which the counts-phase mask would drop, and other *-gli* words are 0.19% of tokens. (2) SRP A, text O8: the vowelless prepositions *s* and *k* get no syllable and no attachment; 1.18% of Serbian subtitle lines. (3) HUN A, secondary, text P3: the abbreviation *Dr*; 0.15% of Hungarian subtitle lines. (4) VIE, the underthesea configuration only, 9 texts: the read texts write *Đ* as U+00D0 (Latin capital eth), which underthesea silently replaces with U+0110, so its pieces no longer align; absent from the Vietnamese subtitles. (5) YUE, 5 texts, both segmenters: U+FE56 SMALL QUESTION MARK isn't phrase punctuation, so whole sentences reach the G2P as one token; absent from the corpora checked (CMN subtitles, YUE Wikipedia). For comparison, the Catalan counts mask drops 2.27% of lines, almost all Stage 2 gap lines; segmentation failures 0.21%, vowelless words 96 lines. Fixes, post hoc in the sense item 5a anticipated (informed by pass/fail, not by NS): (3)–(5) by the normalization rule Brett accepted this morning (an explicit list, mapping to the spoken form, applied before any option counts, every firing logged): U+00D0/U+00F0 to U+0110/U+0111 in VIE, the small forms U+FE50–FE57 to their full-width forms in CMN and YUE, *Dr* to *doktor* in HUN; (2) by a declared counter fallback (Brett: a convention's own rule failing needs one), vowelless SRP proclitics attaching to the following word; (1) is a G2P repair, the narrow case of the Epitran vowel repair Brett dropped on 2026-10-10, so it waits for his word. After the counts phase, each language's mask is read by reason, and any reason at 1% of lines or more is looked at.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: add the Italian *-gli* repair** ("add it"; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude; post hoc, informed by the read-text pass/fail check, not by NS). One G2P rule for ITA: a word whose spelling ends in *-gli* and whose Epitran pronunciation ends on /ʎ/ gets its /i/ back (*gli* /ʎi/, *figli*, *degli*); every firing counted. The narrow exception to the 2026-10-10 drop of the Epitran vowel repair, which stands for the other cases (*sciare*). The other four fixes from the check (VIE, CMN/YUE, HUN normalization; the SRP proclitic fallback) go in with it, in a working-copy branch, under one Codex review with the token-file lifecycle, the zero-backoff handling and the onset lexicon; the check is rerun in main after the import.

2026-10-15 — **Brett: add the Italian *qu* repair** ("add it"; decision owner: Brett; assisting: Claude; post hoc, found by the read-text fix agent, not by NS). Epitran reads the *u* after *q* as a vowel, and at word end every Italian option keeps it as a nucleus: *qui* and *qua* count 2 (should be 1), *acqua* and *cinque* 3, *comunque* 4; word-internal *qu* already counts right (*quando*, *questo* 2). Words ending in *qu* plus a vowel are 0.55% of Italian subtitle tokens (*qui* 0.38%), about a quarter of a percent of Italian syllables, the same error in every universe; unlike the onset choice it changes counts. The repair, in the same place as the *-gli* one: the *u* after *q* is never a nucleus (it is read as the glide), every firing counted. A second narrow exception to the 2026-10-10 drop of the Epitran vowel repair, which stands for the other cases.

2026-10-15 — **Escape coding: defaults logged before it is built or run** (Claude, under Brett's OOV rule of today, which makes escape coding the main analysis and the pooled unknown symbol the labelled secondary; the choices below move a result far less than the rule itself, so they are defaults, not questions). (1) What is charged: every scored target that the pooled symbol stands for, a syllable relabelled `<rare>` on any rung and, on the flagged rungs, also a pair target `b<flag>sp` whose syllable is not itself pooled, pays the model's bits for the pooled target plus the bits to spell its original label; nothing else changes. (2) The spelling model: the label's Unicode code points as stored (NFC), then an end symbol; interpolated Witten–Bell over the two previous characters, down to add-one over the training alphabet plus an end symbol and one unknown-character symbol; fitted per declaration and budget on that budget's training hapax types (what the pooled symbol stands for in training), one count each, with the alphabet from every training label. (3) Where: computed at scoring time from each preparation's syllable record through the same cut and pooling (`prepare` and the counts phase are unchanged, so they need not run again for it), per job, rung and test document; the entropy phase assembles, reports and runs the plateau check twice, the escape-coded scores as the main analysis and the pooled scores labelled as the secondary. The training plug-in endpoints (rungs 0 and 1w) are left as they are. (4) Size: the pooled held-out share is 0.1–0.5% of tokens in the Catalan counts and a spelled syllable costs on the order of 15–20 bits, so escape coding adds roughly 0.02–0.1 bits a syllable against the pooled scores, and another reasonable spelling model would move that by a small fraction of it. Earlier entry today: "`prepare` runs again under it" holds only in that the counts phase has not yet run to completion on any corpus; escape coding itself does not need it.

The escape-coded and pooled scores themselves will give the size.

The post-fix Catalan run spent most of its CPU starting espeak-ng again for the same word types in nine cache folders (espeak-ng costs about 12 ms of CPU a call, two thirds of it kernel time; `results/p9/README.md` §1); now every stage, variant, declaration and run of a language shares `counts/g2p-cache/<LANG>`. With INFO_RATE_WORKERS above 1, first-listed variants are measured first and then the rest, every variant is prepared at once, and each language's entry is assembled in variant order; worker usage records add child CPU. 2026-10-16 — **How the one-at-a-time sensitivity checks run: design logged before building** (Claude; the checks are Brett's, 2026-10-10 and 2026-10-15; these choices decide no result). (1) What runs: every boundary check (`multiverse.checks`, 35) and every preparation check (`preparation_checks`, 33: the onset source and floors 1 and 10 in the 11 languages that learn onsets). (2) Everything else at default: the main run's first-listed text variant, budget B only, the first-listed line treatment only (running text), KenLM only. (3) Comparison within the check, on identical text: a boundary check is one preparation holding the option's default declaration and the changed one; a language's three preparation checks share one default-settings preparation of its first-listed declaration. Each check preparation has its own mask, over its own declarations, recorded as a view of the registry (as `include_calibration` already adds FIN-C), so every existing mask and preparation check applies unchanged; lines either arm fails are dropped for both, and the check's masked-line count is reported beside the main preparation's. (4) Where: the counts phase prepares them as a third run beside main and Wikipedia, so the stamp binds their preparations; the entropy phase scores them in their own folder, outside the main assembly. (5) Report per check: both arms' estimates at every rung and their difference, the share of syllable labels that differ position by position (or the count difference where a row changes counts), and the mask line counts. (6) Cost: 114 declaration scans (about the main variant's own prepare) and 114 KenLM jobs beside the main run's 712; CPU hours from the `CAT-4` measurement.

The spelling model's reach, from counts only: uniform spelling of every pooled target (about 27 bits each) would add at most 0.063 bits per held-out syllable, and a change of spelling model moves part of that, of order 0.01 bits, which is 1% of ID only near 1 bit per syllable; so the spelling-model choices are not brought to Brett, on Catalan's numbers (the about-1% rule still applies to the other languages' counts). An earlier version of this bound in the session ("a whole spelling costs well under 30 bits") was wrong, since uniform spelling costs about 27; the conclusion is unchanged. 2026-10-16 — **Stress checks reported as unmeasured where the G2P writes no stress** (Claude; under Brett's rule of 2026-10-10 that unmeasured rows are reported as such; working copy 9a5da9b). Row 51 swaps unmarked for marked stress. For HUN, ITA, SRP and TUR the production G2P is Epitran, whose maps and rules for these languages contain no stress mark, so no phone carries stress and both arms are the same text: run as checks they would show 0% of labels changed and a zero difference, which reads as "stress makes no difference" where the alternative can't be expressed. They join row 52 (Korean whole-phrase G2P) as not run, with the reason, and are not prepared (seven check preparations fewer). FRA keeps its check (its espeak fallback marks stress; 1.4–1.6% of labels changed in the rehearsal). A test reads the Epitran files so that a tool upgrade that adds stress would fail it. Raised by a side note (2026-10-16) after an outside review found it.

2026-10-16 — **Preparations at real size: per-document data stored once, jobs by reference, the entropy phase one preparation at a time; design before building** (Claude; follows the CAT-4 entry's manifest finding; result-neutral, so Claude's to choose under Brett's standing guidance; one outside review). Sizing from CAT-4's `inputs.json` (217 MB at B: per-document token-file records 89%, word corpora 6%, jobs 10%), projected to Italian Wikipedia 3B (36 declarations, about 32,000 documents, about 1,300 of them test documents): dropping only the training and validation documents' records, as the CAT-4 entry planned, would leave about 1.7 GB, half of it in the jobs, because every job repeats the preparation's per-document lists (its documents, their source digests and realised counts, and each test document's file at every rung). The entropy phase also loads every preparation's jobs at once, about 10 GB of JSON for the KenLM jobs over the run, and a JSON load takes about six times its size in memory; this laptop has 32 GB. The change: (1) per-document token files are recorded for test documents only; training and validation documents are read only through their joined split files, which stay recorded. (2) The per-document digests and sizes that remain (test documents' token files, word corpora) go in compact tables per declaration, treatment and rung, in document order, and their paths follow from the document. (3) Jobs are stored without their per-document fields, which are rebuilt on reading from the manifest's own (sharing its objects in memory) and checked against the full job's recorded digest; `jobs.json`, a copy of the manifest's jobs, is no longer written. (4) One reader verifies a manifest and keeps it, for the runner's provenance check, materialisation, escape coding, the checks and the entropy phase, in place of each parsing it per job. (5) The entropy phase reads, checks and runs the KenLM jobs one preparation at a time; the cross-job checks still span every preparation (one mask per language and text settings, one set of source documents per language and settings, no duplicate jobs). Unchanged: job identities, score records, estimates. Verified by a full rehearsal compared with `.p9-checks-all` (`tests/p8_compare_runs.py`), in which only the dropped records and the seven stress checks may differ. Not changed here: each score record and the active run manifest still carry the job's documents and source digests, so the assembly holds about 6 GB of JSON at real size, too much for this laptop and comfortable on a large cloud machine; that figure goes into the local-or-cloud decision (item 6).

2026-10-16 — **Correction to the entry above (preparations at real size): the shares of CAT-4's `inputs.json`** (Claude). 89% is all file records together (`artifacts` 105 MB and `artifact_bytes` 88 MB of 217 MB); per-document token files are about 83% (266,623 of 286,185 records), word corpora about 6%, jobs about 10%. The design is unchanged.

2026-10-16 — **Escape coding and the one-at-a-time checks imported into main** (Claude; working copy 035f7cf on b2a0663; each had one outside review, `review-p9escape-20261015` and `review-p9checks-20261016`). Files: `src/stage4/escape.py`, `src/stage7/sensitivity_checks.py` (new), `src/stage3/freeze.py`, `src/stage3/multiverse.py`, `src/stage4/linguistic_inputs.py`, `src/stage4/pipeline.py`, `src/stage7/counts_phase.py`, `src/stage7/entropy_phases.py`, and tests `test_p9_escape.py`, `test_p9_checks.py` (new), `test_p7_entropy_phases.py`. Drift check: every modified file in main equalled the branch base. Rehearsals in the working copy, both exit 0 on invented text with failing lines: `.p9-escape-all` (the OOV rule chose pooled there, the invented text having no unseen syllables, so escape coding ran as the full secondary; 6.3% of targets pooled) and `.p9-checks-all` (11:08 to 15:57: 712 KenLM and 22 neural jobs, 49 groups, 4,928 plateau rows, the same two 3B settings omitted at the working-copy size cap as in every rehearsal since `.p8-final2`; 78 check preparations, 11 default views and 67 checks, every arm scored, row 52 reported as not run). Also in this import: the checks report names each row's default declaration, since several rows share a check (one per declaration with the setting); re-running the report on the rehearsal's records gives the same 67 rows. Main's full suite: 90 files, 1,021 tests, 0 failures, 2 skipped.

2026-10-16 — **Correction to the import entry above (escape coding and the checks): what the size cap stops** (Claude). The two 3B settings are not omitted at the working-copy size cap: they are omitted because fewer than two invented languages reach 3B in the rehearsal (`issues`: "fewer than two available language caches"), as `results/p8/final-rehearsal/README.md` says. What the cap stops is the main report: `assemble.write_outputs` projects its complete ranked curve over the 1.6 million universes at about 10 GB, over the runner's default 3 GB limit, and stops before writing (`report.stopped`, "working-copy stop: projected size exceeds 3221225472 bytes", in `.p9-checks-all`, `.p9-escape-all` and every full rehearsal since P8's final one). So no rehearsal since then has written the main report, only the plateau and checks reports, and nothing in production sets a larger limit: the real run would end without its main report. Found 2026-10-16 while sizing parallel KenLM scoring; the fix (a production limit for the report and the curve written compressed, checked by a rehearsal that writes the report) is result-neutral and Claude's to choose, with one outside review together with parallel KenLM scoring.

2026-10-16 — **Entropy phase at real size: KenLM jobs scored several at a time, and the main report written; design before building** (Claude; result-neutral, so Claude's to choose under Brett's standing guidance; Brett asked what could be sped up; one outside review). KenLM: the fake-data runs at 10 million tokens (`results/fakedata/f1c_kenlm_10m.txt`, 600 types) took 1,600–1,700 s for eleven of a job's fourteen models, so about half an hour a job at B, and the 712 KenLM jobs and 60 checks come to several hundred CPU hours (a floor: real vocabularies are larger), which the entropy phase runs one at a time. The change: (1) with `INFO_RATE_SCORING_WORKERS` above 1, each KenLM job (and each check arm) runs in a fresh interpreter in its own folder, as the checks and the counts phase's workers already do; the driver makes the cross-job checks over every job first, as now, then keeps that many jobs running; each worker rebuilds its job from the manifest, materialises it, runs it, removes its files and writes its escape table; the driver links each job's cache record into the phase's cache and writes one active manifest with every job in job order, so the assembly reads what it reads now. A stop in any job starts no further jobs; every worker must report the same authorisation; with a custom estimator (simulated runs only) or one worker, the phase runs in process as now. Concurrent jobs each keep room for the others' declared peaks above the free-space floor. The number of workers is set by memory (each job's materialisation decodes its declaration's whole syllable record, GBs at 3B), not by cores. (2) The main report: `write_outputs` stops before writing anything when its projected size passes the runner's default 3 GB limit, which the complete curve over 1.6 million universes does (about 10 GB projected); the entropy phase now gives it its own limit, 40 GB, under the same free-space floor and `data/` ceiling. Verified by unit tests (two workers against one, native KenLM, on the lifecycle fixture) and a full rehearsal with two scoring workers that must write the main and secondary reports, compared with `.p9-scale-all`.

2026-10-16 — **Removal masks scanned a declaration per worker** (Claude; result-neutral, so Claude's to choose under Brett's standing guidance; Brett asked what could be sped up; logged as built, one outside review; working copy 504f944). By the per-stage projection an Italian text variant is about 119 CPU hours in one process, 72 of them in `build_language_mask` (the measure stage's counts mask and `prepare`'s removal mask), which scans every document under each of 36 declarations in turn. With `INFO_RATE_SCAN_WORKERS` above 1 the declarations are scanned that many at a time in fresh interpreters and merged into the same mask (each declaration's configuration table and problems are independent of the others'); a worker holds its documents and one scan at a time, so this costs little memory. Production masks are now built from each onset inventory read back from its file; a test requires the same mask digest from the in-memory inventories, from the files with one worker and with two. `prepare`'s per-declaration scan (about 41 of the 119 hours) is not split: each worker would hold a declaration's scanned text (8.5 GB at Catalan B), so its worker count is a memory decision for the machine chosen under item 6. Also on this branch, the four fixes to the parallel-scoring review (`review-p9kpar-20261016`), including the driver's manifest writes under the run's limit and the declared KenLM peak raised to 40 times the largest training rung file after a B/2 measurement (`results/p9/kenlm-peak/`).

2026-10-16 — **KenLM test documents scored with one model load and one ARPA pass per rung** (Claude; result-neutral, so Claude's to choose under Brett's standing guidance; logged as built, one outside review; working copy 408ccab). Found while sizing the entropy phase: each test document was scored with its own `score` call, and for the six window rungs of order 3 to 5 each call read the whole ARPA in Python and loaded the model again. With 126 (Catalan B) to about 1,300 (Italian Wikipedia 3B) test documents and models of 1.5 to 2.5 GB, a KenLM job would have taken tens to hundreds of hours, so the entropy phase would not have finished at real size; the fake-data timings behind "half an hour a job" had a few test documents. Now each rung's model, unigrams, window masses (over every document's histories) and normalisers are built once and used for each document. Each history's mass depends only on that history and its suffixes, so every score is unchanged; a test fitting KenLM on invented text requires identical per-document scores with and without the shared context, and the run comparison against the earlier rehearsals checks scores too. Also on this branch: the split-mask review's fix (documents cross to the workers in order, so the configuration tables and the mask digest don't depend on the worker count).

2026-10-16 — **`prepare`'s declaration scans in workers** (Claude; result-neutral, so Claude's to choose under Brett's standing guidance; logged as built, one outside review). With `INFO_RATE_SCAN_WORKERS` above 1 (and no supplied G2P), each declaration's scan of the needed text runs in its own process and writes its syllable record; `prepare` then reads each record back, as materialisation and escape coding do, and goes on as before. About 28 of an Italian variant's remaining 41 single-process hours. Each such worker holds a declaration's scanned text (8.5 GB at Catalan B, probably about twice at 3B), so on any machine the worker count is set by memory; on the laptop it stays at 1. A test requires the same records, counts, file tables and jobs with one worker and with two.

2026-10-17 — **German 3B probe recorded: memory per `prepare` worker where a corpus reaches 3B** (Claude; `results/p9/deu-3b-probe/`; for item 6). German subtitles at the projected B₀ (8.26 million syllables), B/2, B and 3B prepared together: `prepare` peaked at 8.0 GB and the measure stage at 2.8 GB; 9.0 CPU h, within 8% of the per-stage projection (3% for the measure stage). So a 3B preparation is not heavier per worker than Catalan's 8.5 GB at B and B/2, against the earlier guess of about double. The run shared the laptop with rehearsals and other work (swap up to 67 GB), so the peak may be understated; it sizes a cloud machine at roughly 8 to 9 GB per counts-phase or scan worker. Held-out OOV at the projected B₀: 0.039% (3B), 0.088% (B), 0.151% (B/2) of syllables, so German B/2 is over the 0.1% line as Catalan's was; escape coding is already the main analysis.
