Before the freeze is written there: (1) the full suite; (2) a full rehearsal on the server compared with the laptop's `.p9-stack-all` by content (`tests/p8_compare_runs.py --dump`), which runs every language's tools on invented text; (3) pronunciation parity on real words: the same sample of frequent word types per language transcribed by each language's production routes on both machines and compared word by word (a count on real corpora, allowed under the NS hold); (4) the read-text check (every read text passes in every configuration) run again on the server. Any difference is diagnosed and brought to Brett if it could move a result by about 1%, before anything is frozen.

The real run is wholly on the server, so a difference from the laptop doesn't bias it; what the checks guard against is a broken or misconfigured tool there, and a read text that fails after the stamp. Kept: the read-text check on the server (minutes; it guards the one irreversible failure), the pronunciation parity scan (about ten minutes for all languages; `scripts/cloud/scan_parity.py`), and the suite. The full rehearsal on the server stays as a smoke test of the whole pipeline on the new machine (about five hours), not as a requirement that its scores equal the laptop's. 2026-10-17 — **F2 declared, before any F2 code or result** (Claude, applying Brett's decisions of 2026-10-15 on F2; `notes/f2-declaration-draft-2026-10-14.md`, now declared). Defaults for what those decisions left open, each a fake-data design detail: lines of 16 fine units instead of 15, so pairs tile each line; Sol's process run as the lag-2 copy chain on a uniform binary inventory with ε = 0.2 (identical to X<sub>i</sub> = X<sub>i−2</sub> ⊕ E<sub>i</sub>, E ~ Bernoulli(0.1)); the Zipf processes at F1's settings (2,000 types, exponent 1.1, ε = 0.3), independent, lag 1 and lag 2; the partial-merge class A the types ranked 5, 10, 15, … (14.7% of the mass; {1} for Sol's process) absorbing the next unit in the line; the deletion class D the types ranked 10, 20, … (6.3% of the mass), not run on Sol's process; KenLM orders 1–9 on fine units and 1–5 on coarse units, scored normalised as in production; one 1M-unit test set per process and line length; the matched span counts fine positions covered by the coarse context, deleted ones included. The oracle is exact given the context KenLM sees, with two approximations checked by simulation before the grid runs (the stationary distribution at every line position when k = 0; the line end ignored for deletion). The draft's comparison with F3's S1–S2 gap is dropped (F3's contrasts are on R<sub>k</sub>'s scale, not bits per unit); the percentage of the true density is reported instead. KenLM runs on the laptop; the neural subset is costed for Brett before anything launches.

2026-10-17 — **F2's oracle checked before the grid: both declared approximations under 0.1%** (Claude; `results/fakedata/f2/f2_oracle_check.tsv`, `src/fakedata/f2_conventions.py`, `tests/test_f2_conventions.py`, 7 tests). On 10M simulated units with a four-type inventory (lags 0–2; lines of 16 and 1,000; k = 0–3), the oracle's cross-entropy minus the plug-in conditional entropy is never negative and is at most 0.0012 bits (0.06%) for the partial merge (lag 2, k = 1, lines of 16: the stationary distribution at the window's start) and under 0.0005 bits for deletion (the line end ignored); the larger gaps for pairs at k = 2–3 (up to 0.0098) match the plug-in's own bias from sparse contexts. Found and fixed during the check, before any F2 result: a class-A unit can stand alone only at its line's last position, so the first oracle used knowledge of the line end that KenLM's context doesn't give (0.08 bits per unit too low at lines of 16); the oracle now mixes over whether the next unit is the line's last, weighted by each position's chance of being a unit boundary. The declaration's oracle wording covers this as part of "given exactly the context KenLM sees".

2026-10-17 — **F2's fine-unit orders stop at 6, before any KenLM result** (Claude; tool limit found in the smoke cell). This machine's KenLM is built with its default maximum order of 6, so `build_binary` and the Python module refuse order 7. KenLM isn't rebuilt (its binaries are in the freeze's environment record, and production uses orders up to 5), so fine units run at orders 1–6 (k = 0–5) and the matched-span comparison is reported only where the matching fine k is 5 or less (for pairs, coarse k ≤ 2 at lines of 16). The same-k comparison (k = 0–4) is unaffected. The smoke cell (lag 2, lines of 16, 8M units, orders 1–6) took under a minute, so the grid is about 3 CPU hours.

2026-10-17 — **Report-step rehearsal at full scale: the main report is written, the secondary report ran out of memory; fixed** (Claude; rehearsal on invented text, the P9 stack's caches, launched 06:03 outside the session). The main report was written in full under the run's own size limit (08:44–08:55; 14 GB: a 1,600,259-universe specification curve of 5.4 GB, a 4.8 GB continuity table and a 4.4 GB summary). Assembling the secondary report then grew the process to a 52 GB footprint on this 32 GB machine, swapping and stalled for two hours with nothing written, so Claude stopped it at 10:50. Cause: the report loop kept the main analysis's assembled groups, and both analyses' records, in memory while the secondary was assembled. Fix in `src/stage7/entropy_phases.py`: each analysis's records are released once its report is written, only the group count and issues are kept, and the calibration universes are taken from the main groups inside the loop; the run record's fields are unchanged and the entropy-phase tests pass. The rehearsal's three large report files were deleted after a sanity check (row counts, a well-formed summary); its scored caches are kept so the report step alone can be rehearsed again once memory allows. For sizing the real machine: one report assembly needs about 14 GB here, and the two reports take about 28 GB of disk.

2026-10-17 — **Correction to the entry above** (Claude). "About 14 GB" for one report assembly came from P8's final rehearsal (14.2 GB peak), not from this run. This run's timing wrapper recorded a maximum resident set of 16.9 GB over its 4 h 43 min, bounded by physical memory (the footprint reached 52 GB with compression and swap); the main report's own peak wasn't measured separately.

2026-10-17 — **F2 after its outside review, before any F2 result was read** (Claude; review `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-f2-20261017/`, Codex read-only, transcript audited, no read outside the payload). The first grid finished before the review returned; its cells and summary were deleted unread. Two findings, both fixed: (1, high) unseen coarse units (about 10–15% of test pairs from a 2,000-type inventory at 4M units) got the unknown symbol's probability while that symbol was left out of the normaliser, so KenLM's arm wasn't a distribution over units. F2 now handles them as production does under Brett's OOV rule: a unit seen once in a cell's training text, and any test unit outside the rest, become the pooled symbol `<rare>`; the escape-coded density (main) adds production's spelling cost (`src/stage4/escape.py`, `Spelling`), each coarse unit spelled from its fine units, one character per fine unit; the pooled density is reported beside it as the secondary. (2, medium) the partial merge's oracle weighted line positions by stationary boundary probabilities and a line-end share that ignored the window's contents (0.43% off on Sol's process, lines of 16, k = 1). The R2 oracle is now exact: the window's likelihood under each class configuration of the positions before it is weighted by the exact joint probability of a boundary and that configuration at every line position (`boundary_joint`), and it matches enumeration of every line for three short-line cases to 1e-9 (a test that fails on the old weighting). Only R3's line end remains approximate (under 0.0005 bits in the simulation check). The review ran at the wrapper's default reasoning effort (none), not the high effort earlier reviews used, so the fixed code gets one review at high effort, run beside the grid; if it finds something, the grid (about an hour) runs again.

2026-10-17 — **Restart after a stop, checked for the Alliance's time-limited jobs** (Claude, prompted by a side agent's note via Brett; probe on the counts phase's invented test corpus, scratchpad script, not committed). Stopping the counts phase at its 50th, 125th or 200th of 250 writes and starting it again in the same folder gave a run record identical to a clean run. A restart doesn't skip finished work wholesale: even a rerun after completion repeated 189 of the 250 writes. Per-document outputs with a committed manifest are reused (checked against their hashes), as are the measuring step's counts (keyed) and the pronunciation cache; the removal masks are rebuilt each time. The freeze records paths, Python's version and installed-file hashes but no host name, so cluster nodes sharing one container and the same paths should pass it. Restart cost at real scale is unmeasured; a timed stop-and-restart on one real language (Catalan main, about 6 CPU h) would measure it when a machine is chosen.

2026-10-17 — **F2's second review (high effort): one finding, resolved by labelling** (Claude; `private/reviews/codex-runs-20261009/review-f2b-20261017/`, transcript audited, nothing flagged). R0–R2 sound, including the exhaustive enumeration; R3 sound apart from its declared line end; the pooling rules, spelling cost, count correction and summary formulas sound. Finding: after pooling, KenLM's histories hold `<rare>` where the oracle conditions on the original units, so KenLM minus the oracle also counts the history information pooling discards (lag 2, pairs, lines of 16, 4M, k = 1: the oracle given pooled histories is 26.6% higher). That is production's behaviour too, so the KenLM arm stays as it is; the component is reported as the production estimator's effect (estimation, pooling and spelling together) with each row's pooled share beside it, and a pooled-history oracle isn't built (default; it would split that effect, not change how the matched-count rate is read). Pairs over a 2,000-type inventory pool about 15% of test units at 4M, against held-out OOV under 0.2% in the real corpora so far, so the Zipf rows for R1 are reported as a sparsity stress test, as F3b's skipped family was.

2026-10-17 — **F2's neural subset on Hugging Face, declared before it runs** (Brett: "yes, run it on HF", after Claude costed it at about 42 fits, under $1 for one seed and about $1–2 for three; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude). Design, fixed now: the production neural estimator (`src/stage5/nnlm.py`, `FixedContextLM`) with production's settings (`production.neural_settings()`, the `zero_unigram_cosine5` preset, model seed 0), on Sol's process and lag 2 (the two where alignment matters), every regrouping each has (Sol R0–R2, lag 2 R0–R3), lines of 16 and 1,000, the 8M budget, the three training seeds, context k = 1, 2 and 4 on both fine and coarse units: 126 fits. Training and test text and pooling are F2's KenLM cells' own (same seeds, same `<rare>` rule); validation text is 500k fine units from a separate seed per process and line length, regrouped and pooled with the cell's training vocabulary; the escape bits are joined from the matching KenLM cells, after checking that the pooled counts agree. One L4 job (`pytorch/pytorch:2.14.1-cuda12.6-cudnn9-runtime`, Torch matching the laptop's), code mounted read-only, results copied to the private bucket `BrettRey/info-rate-f3b` under a new label; a 3-hour limit caps the cost at about $2.40. Reported beside KenLM in F2's report, same comparisons.

2026-10-17 — **F2's neural subset split for cost, before any neural result was read** (Claude, after a side agent's note via Brett; the cost duty in DECISIONS.md, 2026-10-11). The estimate Brett approved (about $1–2) took F3b's 44 s a fit, where vocabularies were at most 10,000 units; F2's lag-2 cells at 8M have 222k units after pooling (pairs) and 65k (partial merge), so those fits cost many times more per step, and pairs at production's evaluation batch of 16,384 need about 15 GB for one batch's logits alone. The first job (`6ad3ab1b…`, about three minutes on the L4) was cancelled; its eight Sol fits weren't kept. Now running (`6ad3ac28…`, 2-hour limit): every cell whose vocabulary stays near F3b's (Sol's process, 54 fits; lag 2's fine units and deletion, 36 fits), then one timed fit each of lag 2's pairs and partial merge (lines of 1,000, seed 1, k = 1), recording time and peak GPU memory, or an out-of-memory failure. The remaining 34 pairs and merge fits go to Brett with a measured cost.

2026-10-17 — **F2's KenLM arm: results** (Claude; `results/fakedata/f2/README.md`, `f2_summary.tsv`, `f2_report.md`; code `5531030b915a`; no design change). With exact probabilities, regrouping cancels at matched spans and not at the same nominal k: where a regrouping brings a dependency within reach, the corrected density falls 30–59% below the fine one (Sol's process, pairs, k = 1: −46% and −53%); pairs cancel exactly at matched spans, partial merges nearly (under 0.05% on lines of 1,000) except for a line-end cost on short lines (0.5–1.2% for the Zipf processes, 18–21% for Sol's); deletion removes about 9.5%. KenLM adds an excess that grows with the coarse inventory (0% on Sol's process; +4% to +65% on the Zipf pairs and merges, whose rows pool 2–13% of test units) and shrinks with budget. Seed SDs are at most 0.007 bits. How this bears on reading the matched-count rate (§6) is for Brett; no design change follows from it automatically.

2026-10-17 — **Precision on the entry above** (Claude). "30–59%" is the pairs regrouping (29.5–58.9%); partial merges, which bring a dependency within reach only for some units, fall 15–49% at the same nominal k (lag 2, k = 1: −15% and −18%; Sol's process, k = 1: −31% and −49%).

Any new machine runs the same scan and `--compare` against it before the freeze.

2026-10-17 — **Brett: four reporting choices for the matched-count rate, from F2** ("yes, log all four recommendations"; decision owner Brett; assisting Claude, from F2's KenLM results, `results/fakedata/f2/README.md`). Fixed before any real result; the code they need is built before the freeze.
1. **Read at the longest rungs.** The matched-count rate (§6) is read mainly where context covers the most material, so that unmatched spans move it least: operationally k = 3 and 4, fixed now rather than chosen from the curves (Claude's default for "where the estimates have plateaued"; Brett can change it before the freeze). Lower rungs are reported, with the span beside them.
2. **Span in a common unit beside every rung.** For each language, universe and rung, the report gives the mean number of segments (phones, as the pipeline's syllable labels give them) that the k previous pipeline syllables cover in the test text, so a reader can see when two conventions or two languages are compared at unequal spans. Counts on corpus text only.
3. **Deletion forks are definitional.** Forks whose options delete segments or units (for example FRA-B's elidable schwas and CMN erhua on the corpus side; the list is drawn from the registry before the freeze) are labelled as alternative definitions of the quantity, not as uncertainty around one value; the headline's spread is shown within each of their options as well as across them.
4. **Inventory-driven estimator bias is judged against the neural estimator.** On the universes both estimators score (first-listed and NS-closest), the report gives each language's KenLM-minus-neural ID<sub>k</sub> beside its syllable inventory size and pooled share; an association of ID with inventory size that the two estimators don't share is reported as estimator bias, not as part of the trade-off.

2026-10-17 — **Choices 2 and the k = 4 pairing built, before any real result** (Claude, applying Brett's decisions above; `src/stage4/escape.py` `job_spans`, `src/stage7/entropy_phases.py`, `src/stage6/assemble.py`; tests in `tests/test_f2_reporting_choices.py`, `test_p9_escape.py`, `test_p9_parallel_scoring.py`). Defaults, each logged here because Brett's decisions left them open:
- **Phones aren't counted.** The syllable records the scorers read hold each syllable's label and word owner only, with no segment list, so phones don't come free in any language; counting them would need a splitter per label system (IPA diacritics and tie bars, ARPAbet, jamo), which is the work option (a) costed. The report gives words and syllables; `segments_sum` stays in the schema, empty. A phone count for ENG (ARPAbet) and KOR (jamo) is a small follow-up if Brett wants one.
- **Words covered, and syllables covered beside them.** For each scored target, the words its k previous syllables cover, each syllable counting 1 over the syllables of its whole word, and the number of those syllables (which falls below k near a line or document start). Means per job and rung go into `context-spans.tsv` and the summary's diagnostics, for every analysis selection, in both reports.
- **Counted on the scored text.** Spans follow the scorers' own context rules (`pipeline.encode` for KenLM, `corpus_vectors` and `nnlm.rung_contexts` for the neural model): within lines each line is a group, KenLM keeping a masked line's syllables and the neural corpus dropping them; across lines unmasked lines join and a masked line breaks the context; `1w` sees the previous syllable only inside the word; each document starts afresh. Each job's count of contexts is checked against the targets its escape table counts. Span tables are made where escape tables are, for KenLM in the serial and worker paths and for the neural jobs, and kept under `work/spans`; they don't enter a job's identity, so cached scores stay valid.
- **The k = 4 pairing gives both rates.** The headline block's `neural_pairing` has, for each named universe both estimators score, KenLM's and the neural R at k = 4 and KenLM minus neural, for the matched-count rate (Brett's choice) and the canonical rate beside it, each the estimator's own R for that universe; a note says k = 3 is KenLM only.

Production's pooled shares are under 0.2% so far, about a tenth of F2's. One production difference to measure, not assume: escape coding also charges KenLM's flagged pair targets, which the neural jobs don't have, so a small KenLM-only spelling term sits inside KenLM minus neural. 2026-10-18 — **Second correction on the KenLM-only spelling term: invented text pools at B/2, and the counts phase's unseen pairs are a lower bound** (Claude; from rehearsal 3's escape tables and `src/stage4/linguistic_inputs.py`). The correction of 2026-10-17 gave the wrong reason.

**What pooling covers.** The pooled symbol stands for every held-out syllable seen fewer than twice in that budget's training text, not only unseen ones (`pool_rare`; Brett, 2026-10-15: "hapax is fine"). On the flagged rungs it also stands for every pair not seen at least twice with an unpooled syllable.

**Invented text at each budget.** At B invented text has no training hapaxes, so nothing is pooled there, by either estimator: in rehearsal 3, 0 of 77,064 KenLM test targets per rung and 0 of 6,732 neural. Its B/2 prefix has many:
- plain rungs: 12.4% of KenLM test targets pooled (9,576 of 77,064), 0.84 escape bits per target;
- flagged rungs: 13.1% pooled (10,104), 0.94 bits per target.

The difference, 528 targets (0.69%) and 0.096 bits per target (about 14 bits each), is the KenLM-only pair term. So the mechanism shows on invented text, but at 12% pooled its size says nothing about the real corpora, where pooled shares are under 0.2% so far.

**What stands.** Neural jobs run only at B, where nothing is pooled, so rehearsal 3 can't show the term inside KenLM minus neural. Its bits still come with the real run.

**Also corrected: where the count comes from.** Each preparation's `pooled.json` holds the term's count (`heldout_pairs`: the flagged targets whose syllable isn't pooled), and the counts phase writes those files. The OOV summary's `unseen_pair_tokens` counts only pairs that training never saw, so it is a lower bound.

The docstring of `tests/run_p8_real_mode.py` ("the B/2 prefix still covers the vocabulary") is true of the vocabulary but not of pooling; it is left unchanged.
